Across all 96 configurations (4 datasets x 4 VLMs x 3 retrieval encoders x 2 prompting strategies), NutriVisRAG reduced the primary estimation error in 56, with improvements more frequent under whole-meal (35/48) than per-item prompting (21/48). Across the evaluated configurations, the lowest NutriVisRAG error was below the best Vanilla error in 13 of 16 VLM--dataset pairs. For GPT-5.4-mini, under whole-meal prompting, the best-performing retrieval encoder for each dataset reduced the primary error on all four datasets relative to Vanilla, with reductions of 1.7--35.7%. Overall, our results show that grounding in multimodal exemplars comprising images and respective annotations can improve nutrient estimation at inference time without task-specific fine-tuning, although the benefits remain configuration-dependent.


### [288] [DART: Domain-Agnostic Residual Transfer for Generalist Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08271)
*Muhammad Aqeel, Maham Nazir, Marco Cristani, Francesco Setti*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Generalist Anomaly Detection (GAD) aims to detect anomalies in unseen domains using only a few reference samples. Recent normal-abnormal guided methods improve this setting by exploiting abnormal references, but their residuals often mix transferable anomaly cues with domain-specific appearance factors such as texture, geometry, and acquisition conditions. This residual domain entanglement limits cross-domain transfer and may produce spurious activations on novel domains. We propose DART, a plug-in framework for normal-abnormal guided GAD that learns to separate residuals into anomaly-relevant and domain-specific components. DART combines a Residual Disentanglement Module, which preserves anomaly-discriminative information while suppressing domain information, with Cross-Domain Meta-Training, which forms episodes across source domains to make the separation effective. The method is architecture-agnostic and can be inserted between residual computation and the detection head of existing GAD pipelines. Experiments on MVTec AD, VisA, and BraTS show consistent improvements over multiple base methods, with the largest gains under stronger domain shifts. this https URL


### [289] [A Proxy-Based Method for Measuring Racial Disparities in~Text-to-Image~Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07132)
*Nia Mucher, Ngan Pham, Jason Chin, Aaron Brown, David Evans*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: We present an automated method for auditing text-to-image generators for representational bias against ground-truth demographic benchmarks. Our approach adapts the racial proxy variables used in U.S. antidiscrimination law to provide a fine-grained comparative analysis of variations in racial representation in generated images. We classify each generated image by race and then compare the model's distribution to publicly available demographic benchmark data to identify patterns of over- and under-representation. We also measure skin tone using CIELAB values and calculate cosine similarity amongst groups of images in order to capture biases that don't show up in demographic comparisons such as differences in image quality across races and skin tones. We report on experiments testing three models (Imagen 4 Fast, Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo, and Flux Schnell) using three categories of proxies (geographic, occupational, and surname) and 50 variants within each category. We find notable variation across the models as well as by proxy category. For example, Imagen 4 Fast overrepresents white classified images more than the other models tested and produces images with lighter, more homogeneous skin tones. Imagen 4 Fast also had the largest representational disparity for Black demographics among surname and geographical prompts. Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo produces results that overall diverge most from census data but yield the most diverse range of skin tones. Our method yields a generalizable and legally-grounded auditing tool that surfaces detailed information about model outputs without relying on implicit normative definitions of fairness.


### [290] [Coverage Hides Churn: Auditing and Preserving Conformal Decisions as VLM Label Registries Grow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07502)
*Jai Kumar Sharma, Peeyush Tapadiya*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Vision-language classifiers can learn new labels from text alone, so the label lists they serve keep growing. We show that this growth silently changes conformal decisions about old labels. Common softmax-based conformal scores compare each label with an average over all labels; adding labels changes that average, and with it the score of every old label, even when the model, the image, and the old calibration data stay the same. Recalibrating after the update keeps the coverage guarantee, yet across eleven settings it changes at least one old decision on 53-90% of new images. Coverage cannot reveal this, because it checks only the true label: in five controlled settings that keep the old calibration, so that only the normalizer changes, old-class coverage moves by at most 0.01 while 60-67% of images change. We propose a lifecycle audit that measures these changes alongside coverage and set size, and a simple fix, PrefixCP: each class keeps the label registry that existed when it was added. Its old decisions then never change, each class keeps exact class-conditional coverage under exchangeability, and one prefix scan computes all historical normalizers at once. PrefixCP recovers 40.9-58.5% of the set-size gap between never updating the reference and recalibrating, which gives smaller sets but changes old decisions.


### [291] [TACT-KV: Tri-Axis Cosine Transform for Compressing Volumetric KV Caches in Medical VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07593)
*Chongyu Qu, Ritchie Zhao, Yufan He, Dong Yang, Zhengyi Lu, Junchao Zhu, Tianyuan Yao, Juming Xiong, Junlin Guo, Yanfan Zhu, Yuechen Yang, Daguang Xu, Bennett Allan Landman, Yucheng Tang, Yuankai Huo*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) applied to 3D medical scans face a structural memory wall. A single CT or MRI volume produces tens of thousands of vision tokens, and the resulting key-value (KV) cache scales linearly with both batch and context. Existing KV compression methods operate on the cache's feature or token statistics, or apply 1D and 2D transforms, leaving the three-axis spatial structure in the cache unexploited. We propose TACT-KV (Tri-Axis Cosine Transform), a calibration-free KV compression method that exploits strong spatial correlations in visual KV states along all three anatomical axes of 3D scans. This correlation concentrates the KV cache energy under a frequency-domain transform. TACT-KV applies a separable 3D discrete cosine transform to the KV cache, allocates a per-layer bit budget across frequency bins via reverse water-filling (more bits to high-energy components), and quantizes each bin with a fixed scalar codebook. We derive a close-form relation: the distortion reduction from adaptive bit allocation is dominated by the spectral concentration of the transformed cache. Across six medical VLMs and two 3D CT benchmarks, TACT-KV achieves the lowest 1-bit prediction-flip rate on eleven of twelve model and dataset pairs in our main comparison. The same advantage reproduces on four general-purpose VLMs evaluated on video tasks, indicating that the benefit of exploiting three-axis structure generalizes beyond medical imaging. At equal high-bandwidth memory (HBM), TACT-KV scales the decoding batch size on a single GPU by up to $8\times$.


### [292] [Multimodal Information Interactions Bottleneck for Multimodal Self-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07718)
*Liangjian Wen, Jiangtao Zheng, Jiang Duan, Qun Dai, Yong Dai, Jianzhuang Liu, Jun Wang, Shu-Dong Huang, Xinwang Liu, Zenglin Xu, Zhao Kang*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Multimodal self-supervised learning is challenged by heterogeneity, unequal informativeness, and complex information interactions across multimodal data sources such as images, text, audio, and tabular data. While contrastive methods effectively align shared semantics, they typically rely on strong multi-view redundancy assumptions, overlooking modality-specific and synergistic information, thus leading to suboptimal representations. We propose the Multimodal Information Interactions Bottleneck (MIIB), a principled framework grounded in Partial Information Decomposition that explicitly encodes redundant, unique, and synergistic information within multimodal interactions. MIIB employs a dual-path model architecture for modality-specific encoding and multimodal fusion, optimized using surrogate tractable entropy-based objectives that enforce minimal sufficient representations without requiring explicit mutual information estimation. By leveraging variational bounds and score-based entropy estimation, MIIB efficiently captures complex cross-modal interactions while suppressing superfluous information. Experiments across diverse benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over state-of-the-art multimodal self-supervised methods. Code is available at this https URL .


### [293] [Enhanced MRI brain tumor detection using deep learning in conjunction with explainable AI SHAP based diverse and multi feature analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07843)
*Asif Rahman, Maqsood Hayat, Nadeem Iqbal, Fawaz Khaled Alarfaj, Salem Alkhalaf, Fahad Alturise*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Recent innovations in medical imaging have markedly improved brain tumor identification, surpassing conventional diagnostic approaches that suffer from low resolution, radiation exposure, and limited contrast. Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) is pivotal in precise and accurate tumor characterization owing to its high-resolution, non-invasive nature. This study investigates the synergy among multiple feature representation schemes such as local Binary Patterns (LBP), Gabor filters, Discrete Wavelet Transform, Fast Fourier Transform, Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN), and Gray-Level Run Length Matrix alongside five learning algorithms namely: k-nearest Neighbor, Random Forest, Support Vector Classifier (SVC), and probabilistic neural network (PNN), and CNN. Empirical findings indicate that LBP in conjunction with SVC and CNN obtained high specificity and accuracy, rendering it a promising method for MRI-based tumor diagnosis. Further to investigate the contribution of LBP, Statistical analysis chi-square and p-value tests are used to confirm the significant impact of LBP feature space for identification of brain Tumor. In addition, The SHAP analysis was used to identify the most important features in classification. In a small dataset, CNN obtained 97.8% accuracy while SVC yielded 98.06% accuracy. In subsequent analysis, a large benchmark dataset is also utilized to evaluate the performance of learning algorithms in order to investigate the generalization power of the proposed model. CNN achieves the highest accuracy of 98.9%, followed by SVC at 96.7%. These results highlight CNN's effectiveness in automated, high-precision tumor diagnosis. Code: this https URL


### [294] [PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07867)
*Jihai Zhang, Yuhan Zhou, Junyou Li, Deheng Ye, Qi Dou, Yu Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Building a playable world requires more than generating plausible video: it demands responsive control, persistent spatial structure, and coherent interactions with dynamic entities. Using generated frames as the sole recurrent world representation ties these requirements to view-dependent appearance synthesis, making it difficult to maintain long-term spatial and temporal consistency in generation and interaction. We present PlayGen2, a real-time interactive world model that separates learned 3D state evolution from neural rendering. PlayGen2 maintains a persistent world memory comprising static geometry represented by a volumetric occupancy grid and identity-indexed 3D states for the player and other entities. Within a fixed-size local region, its world-state model jointly learns action-conditioned entity dynamics, geometry completion, and entity discovery, committing updates to a persistent world memory. A separate neural renderer synthesizes first-person observations from depth and semantic projections of the world state, without feeding generated images back into state prediction. Using Doom as a testbed, we demonstrate that the model successfully learns world dynamics including geometry-aware player motion and player--NPC interactions. The framework is computationally efficient, enabling the complete simulation and rendering pipeline to achieve approximately 160ms backend latency on a MacBook Pro with an M4 Pro chip. Beyond single-player interaction, we demonstrate zero-shot deployment on unseen Doom maps, multiplayer interaction through the composition of single-player models, and programmable gameplay through rule-based state interventions. These capabilities demonstrate the transferability and extensibility of the learned game-world model. Code and pretrained model weights are publicly available.


### [295] [Improving Context-Shift Robustness of Convolutional Networks via Context-Regularized Cross-Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08157)
*Jinen Setpal, Chaoyue Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Image classification models often encounter objects in diverse and unpredictable contexts at test time, which may differ substantially from those seen during training. This phenomenon, termed context shift, exposes a key fragility in neural networks: when models rely on non-causal contextual cues for prediction, shifts in context can lead to significant performance degradation. In this paper, we propose a novel training framework to improve the robustness of convolutional neural networks under context shift. Our approach leverages spatial feature attribution to guide models toward making predictions that rely less on contextual regions and more on object-relevant features. As a first step, recognizing a theoretical limitation of existing feature attribution methods we introduce a new variant, ContrastiveCAMs, which produces more faithful attribution maps of model predictions. Building on ContrastiveCAMs, we further propose Context-Regularized Cross-Entropy (CR-CE), a modification of the standard cross-entropy loss that regularizes the model's attention by suppressing the influence of contextual regions, thereby improving robustness to context shift. We evaluate the effectiveness of our approach on several medium-to-large scale datasets (Waterbirds, Spawrious, ImageNet/ImageNet-BG, Hard-ImageNet), and report consistent improvements in context-shift robustness.


### [296] [4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08342)
*Adnan Armouti, Guanyue Qian, Rajalakshmi Nandakumar*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. We present 4D Point Splatting (4DPS), a physics-based differentiable renderer that fits an explicit moving scene to a real capture. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our output is the raw complex return a real radar records, so we apply the same signal processing on the rendered data to obtain any representation for training any SOTA downstream model. We fit 235 sequences on HuPR and 100 on RT-Pose, two datasets with different radars. Our reconstructions set the state of the art, with median correlations of 0.940 and 0.830 on range-azimuth, 0.829 and 0.695 on range-Doppler, and 0.988 and 0.966 on micro-Doppler. Our renders also set the state of the art in downstream sim2real gap. On RT-Pose, a pose model trained on them reaches 21.9 cm MPJPE on real data, against 16.7 cm for real training and 127.6 cm for the RF-Genesis baseline, where both the rendered- and real-trained models learn nearly the same features. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.


### [297] [USB Gym: A Gymnasium Testbed for Closed-Loop Ultrasound View Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08352)
*Thanapong Intharah, Zhichao Gao, Yiliang Liu, Hao Dong*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Background and Objective: Our main goal is ultrasound guidance: moving a probe until its image matches a target view. Published methods for each part of a guide are evaluated in separate simulators. We asked whether these methods, combined into one guide, are good enough. Methods: We built USB Gym, a testbed whose standard engine simulates ultrasound from public patient CT, with a network drawing organ textures from CT labels. Its guide has three replaceable parts (slots), an organ segmenter, a controller and a reward, which received 20 published methods, each run on the same 60 episodes of six held-out patients. In each slot, a published method replaced ours only if detectably better on these patients. The better of this combination and our original guide on 20 new CTs is the benchmark baseline. A pose oracle, reading the exact probe pose available only in simulation, set the reference. The results below are exploratory. Results: On these CTs, the probe started on average 16.0 mm from the target view; the pose oracle brought it to 1.5 mm. Of the results known when the combination was fixed, only an image-similarity reward (SSIM) was slightly but detectably better, so the combination was our guide with that reward. This combination was the benchmark baseline, ending at 13.7 mm (16% of the oracle's progress). Conclusions: Combining untuned published methods with ours did not guide the probe near the target view; the task remains under-explored. The testbed accepts new methods; no guide was tested on hardware.


<div id='cond-mat.stat-mech'></div>

# cond-mat.stat-mech [[Back]](#toc)

### [298] [Masked diffusion as random sequential adsorption: an exactly solvable model for parallel decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07845)
*Adrian Baule*

Main category: cond-mat.stat-mech

Abstract: Masked diffusion models generate text by reversing a stochastic masking process. Unlike autoregressive LLMs, which generate text one token at a time, they can reveal many tokens in parallel, at the price of an error, since tokens revealed together are drawn independently. We show that this trade-off maps onto the statistical physics of driven many-particle systems. (i) For any data, the error of parallel generation is the entropy production of the generation process relative to the reversed masking process. (ii) For Markov data, generation is a random sequential adsorption process with labelled gaps, which we solve exactly for any number of steps. (iii) For many steps, the error becomes the dissipation of a slowly driven system: the unmasking schedule plays the role of the driving protocol, the number of steps that of its duration, and the error is set by a thermodynamic metric that we obtain in closed form from the mutual information between tokens. Optimal schedules are geodesics of this metric. The exact solution shows how correlations in the text shape both the error and the quality of the output. The longer the correlations, the more the error concentrates at the start of generation. Schedules that start with small steps then gain more, both in path error and in perplexity, and need fewer steps to reach their asymptotic behaviour.


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [299] [Estimating Spectrum-Free Information Bounds for Generative Surrogates Beyond Conditional Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07826)
*Chaitanya Dumbre, Sourabh Dube*

Main category: hep-ph

Abstract: Conditional generative surrogates in high-energy physics, such as models that predict reconstructed jets from partons, are often evaluated on $1\text{D}$ marginal divergences among other metrics. Taken alone these metrics can reward conditional collapse. On a CMS simulation benchmark, a sampler that ignores the conditioner beats a trained Mixture Density Network (MDN) on all four target marginals under both KL and Wasserstein-1, and cannot be stably separated from a Conditional Flow Matching (CFM) model on two of the four. We instead score the models with the Continuous Ranked Probability Skill Score (CRPSS) from forecast verification, taking the exact marginal as the reference. We show how global skill depends on the evaluation spectrum, and we also report it within bins of a conditioning variable, where that dependence largely goes away. We then introduce a data-driven $k$-Nearest-Neighbor ($k$-NN) extrapolation that estimates the best CRPS the conditioning information allows. Both models agree with this estimate on all four targets, which suggests that current architectures have reached the information available in the conditioner, at least as far as these per-target scores can tell.


<div id='math.OC'></div>

# math.OC [[Back]](#toc)

### [300] [Randomized Parallel Complexity of Weakly Convex Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07675)
*Kang Liu*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: We study the minimum number of query rounds needed to find approximate stationary points of nonsmooth weakly convex functions, measured by the gradient of the Moreau envelope. Each round permits multiple simultaneous queries. In the stated parameter regime, the standard subgradient rate is optimal in sufficiently high dimensions, even for randomized algorithms making polynomially many parallel queries per round. We then show how the order structure of the real line enables faster search: a deterministic algorithm using only function values achieves logarithmic round complexity, with a further reduction through parallel queries. Matching lower bounds hold even when each query returns the entire subdifferential. Together, these results clarify the role of dimension in determining the benefits of parallel queries for weakly convex optimization.


### [301] [Last-Iterate Convergence of Stochastic Gradient Methods for Unbounded Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07334)
*Kevin Kurian Thomas Vaidyan, Ahmet Alacaoglu, Michael P. Friedlander*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: Last-iterate convergence guarantees for stochastic gradient methods have attracted significant attention as practical implementations typically return the last iterate instead of an average of the iterates. In this paper, we study two approaches for analyzing the last-iterate convergence of proximal and projected stochastic gradient methods without bounded domain or bounded variance assumptions, where black-box variational inference serves as a running example. Under two related assumptions relaxing the bounded variance, we show near-optimal anytime rate $\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\sqrt{T})$ for the last iterate. Moreover, when the horizon $T$ is known, we remove the logarithmic factor and obtain the optimal order $\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})$, matching the order known under bounded variance or bounded domain assumptions.


### [302] [From What Is Used to What Is Missing: Rethinking Dynamic Rank in LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07349)
*Peng Xue, Guodong Fan, Guang-Yong Chen, Xing Chen*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning with LoRA is governed by rank, which controls parameter budget and locally accessible task updates. Existing dynamic-rank methods mainly focus on capacity allocation, leaving unresolved whether the current adaptation space is sufficient for the task. We characterize the first-order information boundary of standard factor gradients, showing that their unobservable subspace coincides with the normal space of the low-rank manifold. Thus, factor gradients alone cannot distinguish under-optimization within the current space from task-relevant directions beyond its first-order reach. This motivates a geometric view of rank evolution, where tangent information optimizes existing degrees of freedom and the normal spectrum quantifies the value of new ones. Under a local quadratic model, we show that leading normal singular directions yield the locally optimal rank expansion, with spectral energy determining its gain. Building on this characterization, we propose GEAR, which turns the tangent--normal decomposition into rank evolution through tangent-space optimization, matrix-free normal-spectrum estimation for rank expansion, and task-induced geometric initialization. Across language and vision benchmarks, GEAR achieves the best average performance in all evaluated settings with substantially fewer trainable parameters. On LLaMA3-8B, it raises the average commonsense score from 86.61 to 88.45 while reducing trainable parameters from 42.0M to 14.4M.


### [303] [Randomized and Oracle-Based Feasible Directions Methods for Nonsmooth Nonconvex Constrained Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07691)
*Dan Greenstein, Nadav Hallak*

Main category: math.OC

Abstract: Minimizing nonsmooth nonconvex objectives over general convex sets using no derivatives of the objective, together with structural access to the constraint set, poses a fundamental geometric difficulty: at boundary points, the feasible direction cone may fail to be closed, preventing the construction of finite sets of search directions that capture all feasible descent directions. We develop a feasible-directions framework for problems structured as the difference between a continuously differentiable function and a convex function over a closed convex set, and access the objective without knowledge of its decomposition, through exact line searches along feasible segments. To address the geometric obstruction, we introduce $\eta$-feasible directions, which remain uniformly separated from the boundary of the feasible direction cone, enabling finite-direction search even in the presence of nonlinear constraints. We present two algorithmic realizations of this framework. The first assumes access to an $\eta$-direction oracle and extends feasible-directions methods from linear to general convex constraints. The second replaces the oracle by a noise-augmented randomization scheme that constructs spanning directions from the affine-hull inner normal and almost surely avoids nonsmooth boundary points when initialized outside that set. Under curvature bounds and Lipschitz continuity of the smooth component's gradient, we jointly control the spanning coefficients and feasible step lengths of the oracle construction. This yields finite-time approximate $d$-stationarity along every unit feasible direction admitting a prescribed positive step length. For both methods, we also establish a rate in the proximal directional minimization (PDM) measure, assuming uniformly bounded direction-set sizes. Randomized exploration ensures that, almost surely, every accumulation point is $d$-stationary.


<div id='cs.MA'></div>

# cs.MA [[Back]](#toc)

### [304] [More Agents, More Reliable? System-Level Reliability of Multi-Agent LLMs under Operational-Profile Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07140)
*Burkay Tunçtürk, Koorosh Aslansefat, Yiannis Papadopoulos, Bao Nguyen*

Main category: cs.MA

Abstract: Teams increasingly build software from cooperating large language model (LLM) agents, often assuming more agents mean more reliability. Yet the usual evidence, average benchmark accuracy, ignores the input mix a deployed system meets and the rule merging the agents' answers. Building on HIP-LLM, a hierarchical imprecise-probability assessment of single LLMs, we propose a framework combining it with a Bayesian network of the system's failure logic for multi-agent systems. Agent failure-probability intervals propagate through this network, where the combining rule can itself fail, into a reliability envelope showing which evidence limits it. In a pipeline extracting regulated chemical-hazard statements, majority voting over three models protected against the weakest but did not clearly exceed the best, and a voter failing on 5\% of demands would largely erase its advantage over the middle model. More agents yield a more defensible reliability claim, not automatically a more reliable system.


<div id='q-bio.NC'></div>

# q-bio.NC [[Back]](#toc)

### [305] [A minimal dynamical model of human-AI cognitive coevolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06928)
*Petar Jolakoski, Dimitar Trajanov, Qiyu Sun, Zhen Wu, Ljupco Kocarev*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: The rapid deployment of generative AI raises the question of whether pervasive cognitive offloading drives a measurable, potentially irreversible decline in unassisted human reasoning. We examine this question with a phenomenological dynamical model based on the Cattell-Horn-Carroll distinction between fluid intelligence ($G_f$) and crystallized intelligence ($G_c$). Starting from the discrete-time Bivariate Latent Change Score formalism used in cognitive-aging research, we derive its continuous-time linear counterpart and introduce a fast third variable representing composite machine-intelligence capability. The machine variable enters the human subsystem through Hill-function modifications of the self-feedback and cross-coupling coefficients. We emphasize that our phenomenological model captures both the positive and negative effects of AI on human cognition. By formalizing the qualitative concepts of positive and negative impacts, we introduce definitions for AI-induced cognitive decline and AI-induced cognitive augmentation. A singular-perturbation reduction, justified via Tikhonov's theorem, collapses the resulting three-dimensional system onto a two-dimensional slow manifold. On this manifold we locate two bifurcations of the equilibrium. In one parameter regime, weakening the contribution of fluid reasoning to crystallized knowledge produces a saddle-node bifurcation that eliminates the stable positive equilibrium. In another, reversing the contribution of crystallized knowledge to fluid reasoning creates opposing feedback; increasing fluid self-feedback then reduces damping and produces a supercritical Hopf bifurcation with a stable periodic orbit. We discuss limitations and future directions, interpret our results as a phenomenological account of a coupled socio-technical system, and state the conditions under which each dynamical regime applies.


### [306] [ApproxBrain: Certified Sufficiency Frontiers in Connectome-Constrained Neural Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06929)
*Ali Mahdavi, Azadeh Zamanifar, Amirfarhad Farhadi, Omid Kashefi*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: Background. A connectome specifies the substrate available to a nervous system, not how much of it a given computation requires. Reported compact sufficient circuits are point estimates: one subset, one task, one fidelity threshold, one parameterisation. Methods. We formalise computational sufficiency as a cost-indexed family of neuron subsets conditioned on a task and a tolerance pair (permitted degradation epsilon, permitted failure fraction rho), separate task adequacy from phenotype fidelity, and certify candidates on held-out parameter draws with one-sided bounds rather than reporting a single minimum. We measure three quantities a single circuit cannot express: non-decomposability, degeneracy, and an intervention-derived role taxonomy. Results. On a released walking simulator, the published 148-neuron circuit is certified sufficient with margin (mean Dtask = 0.017 over 32 held-out draws, no failure), no 146- or 147-neuron subset of it is sufficient, and removing any one of its three interneurons destroys adequacy. Complement splitting recovers the published set exactly in three of seven seeds and reaches its size in five, within 66-97 evaluations, against 221 neurons for the bundle hierarchy at the same budget. Varying only the task changes which neurons are needed: our best-found solutions retain 155 and 149 neurons and share only 3 of 11 searchable neurons. Relaxing the tolerance reveals alternative sufficient circuits, and the whole-CNS interface alone fails every replicate while 93 search-chosen neurons give a certified solution. Conclusions. Sufficiency is set-valued and conditioned jointly on task and tolerance, and no single search strategy dominates across regimes. A frontier is interpretable only alongside its interface-only baseline, its tolerance and its budget. Every result here is model-relative.


### [307] [Oscillatory Phase Coding Emerges in Adaptive RNNs for Flexible Working Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06932)
*Kehan Lu, Tianhao Chu, Aiersi Tuerhong, Yiwen Sun, Huan Luo, Fang Fang, Si Wu*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: Oscillatory phase coding (OPC) is widely observed in neural systems and has been implicated in cognitive functions such as memory and sequence processing. However, its computational role and neural substrates remain largely unclear. Here, we studied OPC by training recurrent neural networks (RNNs) with adaptation to perform sequence working memory tasks with variable delays. After training, we found that OPC emerges in the RNN spontaneously: the memorized items recur at distinct phases of a shared rhythm, with oscillation amplitude encoding item identity and phase encoding serial order. Ablation studies identified three key elements required for OPC: adaptation in neural dynamics, variable delay in memory retention, and flexibility in reading out information. Neuron-type analysis revealed the emergence of content and time cells in the trained RNN, and they process item identity and sequence order separately. Recurrent connectivity analysis revealed phase-organized connections among time cells and structured connections within digit-selective content-cell groups. Inspired by these findings, we proposed a neural circuit model which reproduces OPC through interactions between content units and an adaptive time ring. Together, this study reveals how OPC arises from network structures and task demands, offers insights into the implementation and computational mechanisms of OPC in biological systems, and inspires us to develop computational models for human-like cognitive functions.


### [308] [BrainPFN: Amortized Brain-Behaviour Prediction via Prior-Data Fitted Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06934)
*Carlo Alberto Barbano, Demian Wassermann*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: The standard approach to predicting behavioral phenotypes from functional MRI is represented by Kernel Ridge Regression (KRR) applied on functional connectivity (FC) matrices. However, this approach is limited: separate models must be fit and tuned for every score and cohort, they do not transfer across datasets, and accuracy degrades sharply when only a handful of subjects are available, as is typical of clinical or cognitive studies. Despite an increasing interest in developing brain foundation models, none has consistently outperformed KRR on behaviour prediction; most reported gains come from easier targets such as age, sex, or diagnosis rather than continuous behavioural scores. We introduce BrainPFN, a Prior Data Fitted Network for brain behaviour regression. BrainPFN is meta-trained on a domain-specific prior built from ~7k real FC matrices, from which we sample one billion synthetic training samples. At inference, a labelled context is simply passed through the network in a single forward pass, with no per-score or per-cohort refitting required. Across four independent cohorts, BrainPFN beats KRR on small sample prediction, and leads recent foundation models pre-trained at four to nine times larger scale. Our meta-training pool, aggregated from open repositories, is a fraction of the size of the corpora behind competing models, most of which sit behind restrictive data use agreements such as UK Biobank. We release this pool publicly alongside code and checkpoints, showing that a modest, openly available pool can match or beat models trained at Biobank scale.


### [309] [From Maps to Metabolism: Historical Precedents and a Unified Critique of Computationalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06936)
*J. Mark Bishop, Slawomir J. Nasuto*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: Alexander Lerchner's recent work on the 'abstraction fallacy' argues that computational functionalism confuses physical processes with abstract descriptions, and that symbolic computation alone cannot instantiate consciousness. Yet these claims were anticipated by Korzybski's map-territory distinction, Searle's critique of strong artificial intelligence, Floridi's levels of abstraction, Spencer et al.'s observer-relativity arguments, Winograd and Flores's account of computation as socially grounded practice, and Nasuto and Bishop's biological arguments. Synthesizing these contributions, this paper advances a form of non-computational physicalism: consciousness depends upon intrinsically organized, metabolically grounded physical dynamics rather than abstract computation, whilst leaving open the possibility of artificial consciousness. An objection is that this critique targets an outdated computationalism. Mechanistic computationalism, associated with Milkowski, Piccinini, Craver, Bechtel, and Kaplan, treats computation as a property of physically realized mechanisms rather than substrate-independent formalism. Although this represents an important advance, it does not dissolve the abstraction fallacy. Computational attributions remain dependent upon choices of state individuation, system boundaries, and interpretive mapping that are not determined by physical facts alone. Kaplan and Craver's 'model-to-mechanism mapping' constrains computational descriptions without eliminating their dependence upon an external observer. Grounding computation in neural mechanisms does not confer intrinsic significance or the normativity explored by Bickhard and Nasuto and Bishop. The abstraction fallacy persists wherever computational descriptions are treated as constitutive of, rather than descriptive of, physical reality.


### [310] [Reading a neuron's tuning from its correlations with other neurons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06927)
*Francesco Lässig*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: What a neuron represents is usually established by relating its activity to a stimulus. We ask whether the same facts are contained in the correlation matrix of a recorded population, which keeps the pairwise relations among the neurons and discards the stimulus. A transformer that sees only the correlation matrix of 512 sampled neurons, with no labels and no reference population in its input, decodes the preferred orientation of mouse visual cortex neurons from in-vivo MICrONS responses to a mean error of 25° (uninformed: 45°; best constant guess: 41°) and their receptive-field position at R^2 = 0.23; on the digital-twin substrate, 20° and 0.48. Asked whether a neuron prefers horizontal or vertical, it is right for 79% of neurons in vivo and 87% on the twin, against 56% for the majority answer; it does not resolve the obliques. The ordering is recoverable because the relations between orientation classes are close to circulant, which fixes the class structure up to rotation and reflection; the rotation of the frame is recoverable only because of a departure from that symmetry. The departure survives equal representation of every orientation and disjoint training and test stimuli, and it is an anisotropy of the signal correlations: in V1 the neurons preferring the dominant orientation have the most similar responses to one another, and a model population with only that property recovers the frame. Across 33 mice of the Allen Brain Observatory, with relations from drifting gratings, orientation transfers to unseen animals only when a sampled population mixes animals; populations drawn from one animal are not decoded measurably below the best constant, a limit of the diversity of populations one animal provides. For receptive fields, what crosses animals is where each animal's imaged patch looks on the screen, not the layout of neurons within it.


### [311] [Capturing Membrane Dynamics and Spike Timing of Human Neurons at Scale using Neural Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06933)
*Luca Ghafourpour, Cindy Zhao, Valentin Duruisseaux, Bahareh Tolooshams, Philip H. Wong, Costas A. Anastassiou, Anima Anandkumar*

Main category: q-bio.NC

Abstract: Characterizing the properties of neuronal cell types is central to understanding brain function. Yet, biophysically realistic neuron models remain difficult to scale to large populations while preserving the richness of cellular dynamics and within-cell-type variability. We present a cell-type-specific neural-operator framework for scalable neuronal population modeling. Rather than training separate surrogate models for individual neurons, the proposed framework learns a shared operator across multiple modeled neurons from the same cell type, conditioned on biologically interpretable electrophysiological features. Relative to the original single-neuron \texttt{NOBLE} setting, learning across heterogeneous neurons requires a richer electrophysiological conditioning representation and a training objective designed to preserve diverse spiking dynamics. This enables efficient generation of large ensembles of somatic voltage responses while preserving variability across cells and model configurations. We evaluate the approach using membrane dynamics metrics, action potential waveform metrics, spike timing accuracy, input-output firing relationships, and electrophysiological feature distributions. Our framework reproduces subthreshold membrane dynamics, spike waveforms, and firing-rate responses across major cortical inhibitory neuron types, while accurately preserving spike timing despite the sensitivity of threshold-crossing dynamics. The predicted electrophysiological feature distributions show strong agreement with detailed simulator outputs and human experimental recordings, including high explained variability in properties such as inter-spike intervals, firing frequency, action-potential shape, and post-spike recovery.


<div id='physics.flu-dyn'></div>

# physics.flu-dyn [[Back]](#toc)

### [312] [Extracting Single-Mode Rayleigh--Taylor Instability dynamics from data via SINDy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07666)
*Chuanxing Wang, Hui Luo, Guohuai Zhu, Hongbo Cai, Shaoping Zhu, Mingxing Luo*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

Abstract: Rayleigh-Taylor instability (RTI) is of fundamental importance in fields such as inertial confinement fusion and supernova explosions, and its evolution is commonly described by potential--flow theory and buoyancy--drag models. However, both types of models struggle to accurately capture the overshoot behavior of bubble and spike velocities, as well as the subsequent reacceleration stage. To address these limitations, we develop a physics-constrained, data-driven framework based on the integral formulation of Sparse Identification of Nonlinear Dynamics (SINDy), which extracts low-dimensional dynamical equations for bubble and spike evolution directly from data. By introducing the nondimensional initial amplitude $A_0 = k\eta_0$ as an explicit parameter, we identify distinct dynamical equations for the bubble and the spike based on data spanning multiple Atwood numbers and initial amplitudes. The resulting low-dimensional dynamical equations not only reproduce the overshoot behavior of the velocity with good accuracy, but also capture the reacceleration stage well, while maintaining high predictive accuracy at unseen values of the wavenumber, acceleration, and Atwood number. This work provides a physics-constrained, data-driven framework complementary to conventional potential-flow and buoyancy--drag models, accurately describing the dynamical evolution of the bubble and spike at high Atwood numbers, and provides a new perspective for directly extracting the dynamical equations of interfacial instabilities from data.


<div id='math.NA'></div>

# math.NA [[Back]](#toc)

### [313] [Asymptotic-Preserving Tensor Neural Networks for 3D High-Field Kinetic Equations with Applications to Uncertainty Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07364)
*Shi Jin, Tian-ai Zhang*

Main category: math.NA

Abstract: Kinetic equations in the high-field regime describe particle transport dominated by electric-field acceleration and collisional relaxation. These equations are widely used in simulations of plasmas and semiconductor devices. Typical models include the Vlasov--Poisson--Fokker--Planck (VPFP) and Boltzmann--Poisson systems. Their high dimensionality and multiscale nature pose two of the major challenges to accurate and efficient numerical solution. To address these challenges, we propose an asymptotic-preserving tensor neural network (APTNN) method. Specifically, to reduce high-dimensional computational costs and memory requirements, we adopt the canonical polyadic (CP) decomposition structure of TNNs to approximate their solutions. We show that the equation residuals retain this decomposition form, allowing high-dimensional loss integrals to be evaluated through combinations of one-dimensional integrals. The resulting computational and storage costs are reduced from exponential to polynomial growth with dimension. To improve the prediction accuracy in the high-field limit, we enhance the baseline AP loss by incorporating the first moment equation. The added loss term directly constrains the limiting flux closure to reduce its approximation error in the high-field regime. The enhanced AP loss can still be evaluated dimension-wise. Numerical results show that APTNN improves prediction accuracy in the high-field regime with significantly reduced computational costs and memory usage. For a 3D3V VPFP problem with multiple random inputs, training takes less than 30 minutes on a single GPU.


<div id='q-fin.RM'></div>

# q-fin.RM [[Back]](#toc)

### [314] [Credibility Clustering for Experience Rating: An Automated Approach to Risk Group Allocation in Group Life Insurance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07110)
*Thom van Rijn, Frank Weber, Michael Merz*

Main category: q-fin.RM

Abstract: In insurance, experience rating plays a crucial role in mitigating adverse selection by adjusting premiums according to historical claims experience. A key step in this process is the classification of insured collectives into homogeneous risk groups. Within group life insurance, this classification traditionally relies on manual decisions and arbitrary thresholds, which may lead to inefficiencies and inconsistent outcomes. This paper proposes a data-driven methodology for automating risk group allocation based on a combination of the multidimensional Bühlmann-Straub model and k-means clustering -- referred to as credibility clustering. By incorporating credibility weights, the method accounts for the varying statistical significance of experience data and ensuresthat risk classifications are both actuarially sound and practically implementable. Empirical results based on real-world group life insurance data from a major European insurer demonstrate that the proposed framework outperforms the two k-means benchmark variants considered in the empirical application in terms of viability, temporal stability and accounting for statistical reliability.


<div id='cs.IT'></div>

# cs.IT [[Back]](#toc)

### [315] [AI-Native Task-Oriented Semantic Communications over a Relay: Benefits and Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08155)
*Yalin E. Sagduyu, Tugba Erpek, Aylin Yener*

Main category: cs.IT

Abstract: Semantic communication (SemCom) using a relay node is considered, where the relay assists in conveying task-relevant information from a source to a destination, with meaning defined by classification labels. A joint end-to-end design of the source encoder, relay transformation, and destination decoders is developed to optimize both semantic inference accuracy and perceptual reconstruction quality for image data. Relay-assisted schemes are shown to outperform direct transmission, with gains that become more pronounced in low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) regimes across different relaying strategies. Alongside these performance benefits, however, a key security vulnerability introduced by the relay is identified, and a Byzantine attack is formulated in which the relay injects structured, learned perturbations into its transmitted signal. The attack operates on intermediate communication representations subject to channel effects, with perturbations generated through a learned mapping applied at test time. It is shown to significantly degrade semantic inference accuracy at the destination. These results reveal a clear trade-off between SemCom gains and security risks in relay-assisted systems, motivating the need for secure relay designs that account for insider threats.


<div id='cs.RO'></div>

# cs.RO [[Back]](#toc)

### [316] [Efficient Diffusion Policy Fine Tuning with Latent Noise Representation Bridging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07275)
*Yuanchu Liang, Yue Yang, An-Chi He, Hanna Kurniawati*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Pre-trained diffusion and flow policies have emerged as powerful backbones for robotics, yet adapting them to novel data via reinforcement learning (RL) remains computationally expensive, sample inefficient and lacks theoretical guarantees. Prior approaches that fine-tune the initial noise bypass costly backpropagation through time but struggle with the "noise aliasing" problem: there does not exista natural link between the noise space value and the action space value. This paper resolves these challenges by introducing the Latent Noise Linear Markov Decision Process (LNL-MDP). We derive the Bridge Equation for LNL-MDPs which eliminates the noise aliasing problem by aligning values with a shared representation between the noise and the action space. Furthermore, our LNL-MDP fine tuning framework falls under the FineTuneRL theory with near-optimal guarantee. Leveraging these insights, we propose Latent Representation Bridging for Diffusion (LARBRID), an algorithm that efficiently learns the representation to optimize the initial distribution that steers the pre-trained model to act optimally in the environment. It yields up to 2-3$\times$ performance increases on challenging control problems across a diverse suite of robotic locomotion and manipulation tasks. Code can be found at: this http URL .


### [317] [GraspBench: Execution-Aware Cross-Robot Evaluation of 6-DoF Grasp Generation in MuJoCo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07651)
*Abdul Basit, Bibek Poudel, Muhammad Shafique*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Modern 6-DoF grasp generators are often evaluated using grasp scores, dataset-level metrics, or single-robot demonstrations, yet proposal quality alone does not determine whether a grasp is executable by a particular robot. We present GraspBench, a controlled MuJoCo protocol for evaluating complete grasp-generation pipelines after robot-specific inverse-kinematics filtering, motion planning, and fixed approach-close-lift execution. GraspBench evaluates six configured pipelines across four robot embodiments, five diagnostic objects, and a structured tabletop pose grid, yielding 540 scenarios per method and 3,240 method-scenario evaluations in total. Missing outputs are conservatively counted as failures, and success follows the binary lift criterion produced by each method's execution runner. GraspGen achieves the highest overall success rate (73.1%), followed by EconomicGrasp (63.5%), while robot- and object-conditioned analyses reveal substantial variation across embodiments and object geometries. GPG produces the largest native candidate sets but achieves 44.3% success, indicating that proposal volume alone does not guarantee robot-executable grasps. Cluster-aware paired intervals support the principal method differences while not estimating repeated-execution uncertainty. The results characterize complete configured pipelines on fixed scenarios rather than intrinsic model quality or stochastic repeatability, showing that grasping performance depends jointly on the generator, robot embodiment, scene, and execution protocol.


### [318] [Energy-Aware Open-Vocabulary Detection Scheduling for Low-Power Robotic Autonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07663)
*Abdul Basit, Muhammad Shafique*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Open-vocabulary object detection (OVD) lets robots search for run-time object queries, but fixed-rate inference spends embedded compute on low-value frames during idle motion, map revisits, or stable viewpoints. This paper presents an energy-aware OVD scheduler for low-power mobile robots. The scheduler uses odometry, map change, and detector confidence to switch among low-, mid-, and high-power YOLO-World-S modes on a Jetson Orin Nano. We evaluate the energy-quality trade-off on an AgileX LIMO using 40 real navigation trials: two target objects, five placements per target-policy pair, and four perception policies. Across bottle and cup trials, the scheduler records 8/10 mission success compared with 7/10 for each fixed-rate baseline, while reducing mean Jetson input power (a Jetson-side power-draw metric) by 5.2% relative to an 8 Hz high-rate baseline and lowering achieved OVD call rate by 34.0%. It does not minimize mission-window Jetson input energy because longer missions can erase instantaneous power savings. A separate 60 s stationary microbenchmark isolates the perception workload: the scheduler lowers mean power from 8.045 W to 7.301 W and energy from 484.7 J to 438.1 J relative to the 8 Hz baseline. These results support robot-state-aware OVD scheduling as a promising power-management mechanism with competitive task reliability, while showing that mission-window Jetson input energy must be read together with task progress and runtime.


### [319] [Aligning Policy Encoders with a Shape Teacher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08275)
*Madelon Bernardy, Mohammad Mahdi Derakhshani, David M Knigge, Riccardo Valperga, Artem Moskalev*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: 3D-aware representations can improve robotic manipulation by providing geometric information that is robust to changes in viewpoint, object position, and occlusion. However, using 3D inputs at deployment requires additional sensing and processing, making image-based policies more practical. We ask whether an image-based policy can acquire 3D shape information during training while retaining a purely image-based interface at deployment. We propose shape-teacher alignment, a training-time mechanism that uses a pretrained 3D shape representation as an alignment target for an image-based policy encoder. Specifically, we use Point-MAE features from individual objects as targets for corresponding image patches, transferring geometric structure to the policy without changing its deployment inputs or architecture. On LIBERO, shape-teacher alignment produces image representations that are more structured around object shape and corresponding parts, while substantially accelerating policy convergence and improving success rates. For pretrained policies, convergence is accelerated by 4.7x and 5.6x on the Object and Spatial suites, respectively, without requiring the shape teacher or depth input at deployment.


### [320] [VisionSIM: Beyond Idealized Sensors - A Multi-Modal Simulator for Robot Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08368)
*Sacha Jungerman, Kaustubh Sadekar, Avery Gump, Sriram Narayanan, Shantanu Gupta, Bhuyashi Deka, Srinivasa Narasimhan, Atul Ingle, Mohit Gupta*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Robotic perception increasingly relies on heterogeneous sensor suites combining cameras with emerging modalities (event, SPAD, iToF/dToF, and thermal). Yet existing simulators idealize these sensors, hiding critical modality-specific failure modes that limit real-world deployment. We present VisionSIM, a unified, physically grounded simulation platform that elevates sensor realism to a first-class concern by modeling the raw physical measurement process for diverse conventional and emerging modalities. We expose fine-grained control over physical sensor parameters, reproducing failure modes overlooked by general-purpose simulators: pileup and glare for LiDAR; phase wrapping, saturation, and motion artifacts for iToF; refractory drops for event cameras; and history-dependent emission for thermal cameras. This enables principled cross-modality comparison on identical scenes and trajectories. We release the platform and a large indoor dataset with synchronized raw measurements and rich ground-truth annotations.


### [321] [MOMASPLAT: Dynamic Novel-View Demonstration Augmentation for Mobile Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08372)
*Sateesh Kumar, Arnav Balaji, Arpit Bahety, Skand Peri, Georgios Pavlakos, Roberto Martín-Martín*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Visuomotor policies trained through imitation learning are highly sensitive to changes in camera viewpoint. This is particularly problematic for mobile manipulation (MoMa), where robots rarely execute manipulation from the exact same base pose used during demonstration collection due to navigation uncertainty, localization drift, and environmental constraints. Recent approaches use novel-view synthesis (NVS) to augment demonstrations with unseen viewpoints, but they assume static scenes and therefore fail to re-render the manipulated object during the post-contact interaction phase, leaving the most critical parts of the demonstration unaugmented. We present MOMASPLAT, a novel-view demonstration augmentation framework for mobile manipulation that synthesizes full manipulation trajectories from unseen robot base poses, including dynamic object interactions. Our key insight is that standard MoMa data collection naturally provides the multiview observations needed to reconstruct both the static scene and the manipulated object in 3D. MOMASPLAT combines 3D Gaussian Splatting, feed-forward 3D foundation models, and kinematics-based action retargeting to separately reconstruct the scene and manipulated object, render them from novel viewpoints, and generate geometrically consistent synthetic demonstrations paired with transformed actions. We evaluate MOMASPLAT on five real-world mobile manipulation tasks involving both rigid and articulated objects across three scenes, and validate the fidelity of its synthesized demonstrations against ground truth in simulation. MOMASPLAT substantially improves robustness to unseen base poses, more than doubling the average success rate of the strongest baseline and maintaining non-trivial success at large base displacements where all baselines fail, while reducing the need for largescale real-world data collection.


### [322] [Failure Prediction and Classification in Vision-Language-Action Models Via Calibrated Latent Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08425)
*Michal P. Podolinsky, Yunhao Yang, Daniel Little, Hong Lu, Rohan Siva, Olin Goudey, Neel P. Bhatt, Matthias Scheutz, Ufuk Topcu*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable general-purpose embodied intelligence, but their performance is brittle under distribution shift, where unanticipated failures can damage equipment or endanger people. Deploying VLAs in real-world systems therefore requires a runtime monitor that is timely, calibrated, and informative while being efficient enough to run on the edge. Existing methods fail to fulfill these requirements. We present VLA-Tug, a lightweight probe over the latent layers of a VLA backbone that improves early failure detection and bounds the missed-detection and false-alarm rates, with minimal inference overhead. We show that supervising on the pre-failure segment of a trajectory increases early failure detection at matched false-positive rates by up to 30% over the strongest learned baseline. Under shift to unseen tasks, the bounds on missed-detection and false-alarm rates remain valid in up to 86% of runs, versus at most 21% for baselines. Unlike existing methods, VLA-Tug predicts the failure mode to enable corrective actions, raising top-3 accuracy on unseen tasks by up to 44% over a majority baseline. VLA-Tug enables real-time monitoring where the well-timed and informative probe supports lightweight steering that improves task success. We release a dataset of 3,180 annotated manipulation episodes to support future work on latent failure detection in VLAs at this https URL .


### [323] [Detecting Silent Faults at the Environment-Trainer Boundary in Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08144)
*Zuo Gou, Chenhao Lu*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Robot reinforcement learning increasingly runs environments outside the trainer: in another process, on another machine, in another language. Many faults at this boundary raise no error. We make the boundary a wire protocol and ask what notices a fault in it. On Pendulum, HalfCheetah, Hopper and a 7-joint arm (Pusher), we injected up to 23 faults into what crosses the wire and 2 into the trainer's log, from one value to every value. Unless every value was wrong, a learning-curve threshold caught at most one run in ten, and a comparison with ten fault-free seeds at most 21%. A clean evaluation of all 1,060 final policies agreed with the curves: of the 728 boundary-fault runs both curve checks passed, 723 scored no more than three standard deviations below fault-free seeds, though nearly all ended on other weights and, for reward faults, logged other returns. Checks of the run itself, not of its outcome, saw every fault. A step ledger kept on both sides and compared step by step caught and located every fault that crossed, on Pendulum, Hopper and Pusher on one machine and on Pendulum across two, with no fault-free run and, on Pendulum, a median 3% more training time. An episode record kept at the environment caught the faults in the trainer's log. We list such faults found in practice, in Gymnasium 1.3 and our own code and, reported by others, in five multi-agent libraries, and recommend a step ledger and episode counts kept at the environment.


### [324] [La-JEPA: Compact Robot Policies with Latent World Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08156)
*Mateo Clémente, George Z. Xue, Amin Abyaneh, Liheng Ma, Rui Heng Yang, Tongtong Cao, Zhanguang Zhang, Yingxue Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: World-action models adapt pretrained video generators, and vision-language-action models build on pretrained vision-language backbones; both tie policy architecture and inference cost to large transformers designed for generation rather than control. It remains unclear how much these generation-oriented choices affect control. In this work, we introduce Latent Action JEPA (La-JEPA), a compact world-action model whose policy transformer is trained from scratch in the feature space of a pretrained V-JEPA 2.1 encoder. Following Fast-WAM, La-JEPA learns to predict future features jointly with actions during training and generates only actions at inference. Among policies without embodied pretraining, La-JEPA achieves state-of-the-art performance in clean and randomized RoboTwin 2.0 scenes and on LIBERO-Plus with correct instructions, while using far fewer parameters than the strongest competing policies. It runs substantially faster than Fast-WAM at comparable performance and outperforms the closest JEPA-based methods, LeapBot-WA and JEPA-WAM, on LIBERO-Plus and RoboTwin 2.0 without their pretrained policy backbones. On four real-robot tasks, its average success is comparable to pi0.5 and above non-embodied-pretrained baselines. Through a design study of the choices behind La-JEPA, we show that JEPA features outperform video VAE latents, most clearly out of distribution, and that training-time future prediction choices significantly impact the policy even when future prediction is skipped at inference. We correct the LIBERO-Plus instructions, as the officially released evaluator corrupts them for most variants, and re-evaluate several strong open baselines. Without a generative pretrained policy backbone, La-JEPA is better and faster than far larger policies, suggesting that robot foundation models may benefit from architectures designed for control rather than inherited from generation.


### [325] [Transformer-Based Trajectory Prescription for sEMG-Optimizing Robot-Mediated Rehabilitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08295)
*Gabriel Parra, Kimia Khoshnami, Daniel S. Brown, Laura A. Hallock*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Robot-mediated rehabilitation has the potential to deliver high-intensity, individualized therapy, but existing systems (which have not consistently improved outcomes) typically prescribe kinematic trajectories rather than explicitly targeting the neuromotor activations thought to support recovery. To address this gap, and ultimately enable activation-optimizing rehabilitation robot behavior, we present a personalizable transformer-based model mapping individuals' muscle activations (as measured via surface electromyography) to prescribed contact force trajectories when interacting with an end-effector rehabilitation robot. The model uses a transformer encoder-decoder leveraging rotary positional embeddings (RoPE) to generate a latent representation of trajectory-relevant and user-specific neuromotor factors, and augments trajectory prediction with flow matching to produce a distribution over plausible prescriptions biased toward the training distribution. Using data from 16 healthy participants completing 15 isometric trajectory tracking tasks, we evaluate generalization across held-out tasks and users, as well as the feasibility of user-specific fine-tuning within a therapy-session timescale. The model achieves mean trajectory prediction errors of <8% on held out tasks and users, with effectively instantaneous deterministic inference and sub-second sampling of trajectory distributions. An initial test using the model during live rehabilitation tasks demonstrates that, on average, participants reach the target neuromotor activations with just 15% error accumulated throughout the task. These results provide a proof of concept for data-driven, activation-targeted trajectory prescription in robot-mediated rehabilitation and establish a foundation for future validation in clinical populations. Data and code have been open-sourced as part of the OpenRobotRehab project on SimTK.


### [326] [SHORE: Stability-Hybrid On-Policy Reinforcement Learning for Online Bin Packing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08364)
*Chang Shi, Ann Majewicz Fey*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Online 3D bin packing with a processing buffer requires choosing which box to pack, its orientation, and its placement while keeping the stack stable under unknown future arrivals. A collapse ends the episode and forfeits the remaining volume. Existing methods use geometric rules or hard stability masks, limiting how risk can influence the joint choice of box, orientation, and placement. We propose Stability-Hybrid On-Policy Reinforcement Learning (SHORE), which combines a learned stability probability of a placement with an on-policy estimate of its return conditional on stability. A 3D U-Net learns stability from executed outcomes and simulated alternatives, revealing outcomes of actions underrepresented in early rollouts. A discrete on-policy actor--critic uses stability predictions in both its action values and policy prior, with gates that limit the effect of an untrained or changing model. In deployment, SHORE does not require additional search or simulation overhead. In Isaac Gym, SHORE reaches 73\% space utilization by 0.4M environment steps, while the same learner without stability modeling reaches that level at about 1M. On the expanded 60-box set, SHORE achieves 0.734 space utilization versus 0.588 for the GOPT state-of-the-art baseline, with a higher placement-failure frequency (1.03\% vs. 0.63\%), trading more risk for greater packed volume. The results show faster learning and higher utilization on the more demanding realistic box arrival set. Ablations suggest that simulated alternatives provide useful early supervision, while executed outcomes alone sustain later performance. We also demonstrate the method on a real robot to verify the simplicity of integrating the method to real bin packing pipelines.


### [327] [Social Humanoid: Socially Responsive Human-Humanoid Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08407)
*Vongani H. Maluleke, James Ni, Kie Horiuchi, Lea Wilken, Jiahui Lei, Evonne Ng, Roei Herzig, Jitendra Malik, Trevor Darrell, Angjoo Kanazawa*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Humanoid robots are typically trained as solitary agents, leaving them unable to coordinate physically with human partners who naturally anticipate and adjust to one another. To bridge this gap, we present Social Humanoid, a hierarchical system that generates and executes socially responsive whole-body motion on a 29-DoF Unitree G1 conditioned on a human partner's motion. Its high-level policy performs flexible sequence modeling of human and humanoid motion through a shared tokenized representation, generating reactive humanoid motion and anticipating the partner's motion when they are occluded, while a low-level whole-body tracking controller executes the generated motion on the robot. To train the system, we introduce a two-phase adaptive retargeting procedure that retargets one interaction partner to the robot while morphing the other partner to preserve contact dynamics, reachability, and relative body scale. We apply this procedure across five motion-capture datasets to produce a new Human-Humanoid Interaction (HHI) benchmark of 260 hours of human-humanoid interactions. Our results demonstrate that Social Humanoid produces coordinated, human-aligned behavior across diverse interaction types in simulation and the real world. Project page: this https URL


<div id='cs.CL'></div>

# cs.CL [[Back]](#toc)

### [328] [Interactive Backdoor Attacks in LLM-Based Proactive Dialogue Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06969)
*Zhiyang Xia, Yuming Shang, Xi Zhang*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: LLM-based proactive dialogue systems provide goal-directed support through sustained interaction. Fine-tuning their underlying models on externally supplied dialogue data introduces backdoor risks. Existing LLM backdoor studies primarily focus on eliciting an attacker-specified response upon trigger activation. We present an interactive backdoor attack framework that uses training-data poisoning to steer post-trigger, multi-turn interactions toward an attacker-specified objective. Poisoned fine-tuning dialogues implant a trigger-conditioned guidance-policy bias that ordinary user utterances can activate, without deployment-time prompt modification or further attacker intervention. Specifically, we instantiate three complementary attacks under progressively expanded post-trigger interaction settings: (1) Direct Attack, which targets an immediate response deviation upon trigger activation, retaining the conventional trigger--response setting; (2) Constrained Progressive Attack, which progressively advances the attack objective along a predefined multi-turn trajectory; and (3) Unconstrained Progressive Attack, which extends target-directed steering to open-ended interactions with varied user continuations. Using psychological counseling as a representative setting, experiments on two LLM backbones and two dialogue datasets show that all three attacks effectively achieve their objectives under their respective interaction settings while largely preserving normal utility. Separate defense evaluations show that the tested training-data filters retain nearly all poisoned dialogues, while three post-admission baselines leave substantial residual attack-specific success.


### [329] [Reclassification Is All You Missed: Surfacing Variant Reinterpretation Need in Online Health Communities at Population Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07054)
*Kamalakkannan Ravi, Mengshu Nie, Annapurna Poduri, Cong Liu*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: Genetic variants are routinely reclassified in ClinVar after a patient is tested, sometimes from uncertain to pathogenic, yet patient recontact is inconsistent, with no clear duty and responsibility diffused among labs, providers, and patients. Many affected people discuss their results in public online health communities that clinical recontact often does not reach. We built PRISM, an agentic large-language-model pipeline that screens these posts, checks whether the variant was later reclassified, and drafts a disclaimer-bearing outreach message, in a no-contact dry run. Across 3.9 million posts from 32 communities, agent-predicted reinterpretation-relevant discussion is rare (0.288%) but concentrated in specialist genetics communities (6.30% versus 0.10% in general medical-advice communities) and growing fivefold since 2014; PRISM surfaces 123 candidate classification differences, 5 of them from an uncertain submission to pathogenic or likely pathogenic. On a held-out set of 30 real-post drafts, a clinician judged 27 factually grounded and 15 fit to send: factual accuracy is necessary but not sufficient for clinically acceptable outreach. PRISM contributes a population-scale reinterpretation-need map with code and data to be released upon publication.


### [330] [Adapting LLM Judges to Observed Human Annotation Policy through Soft Prompt Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07126)
*Tamás Janusko, Sven Kleinkop, Ricardo Bochnia, Gunnar Hempel, Steffen Tomschke, Jürgen Anke, Maik Thiele*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly employed as automated judges for evaluating chatbot responses. While proprietary models can achieve high agreement with human annotators, they remain costly and difficult to deploy in practical settings. Lightweight open-source LLM judges provide an attractive alternative but typically exhibit lower agreement with human ratings. In this paper, we investigate whether soft prompt tuning can align open-source LLM judges more closely with human annotation policy. We train soft prompts on the FairBotBench benchmark and evaluate the adapted judges across the dimensions of bias, toxicity, and personalization. Our findings demonstrate that hybrid soft-prompt tuning, which combines textual prompts with learned continuous prompt embeddings, consistently improves agreement with human annotations over the corresponding untuned baselines. For Qwen 3.5 9B, the adapted judges surpass both hosted reference judges across all three evaluation dimensions.


### [331] [Measured Coupling for Parallel Decoding in Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08307)
*Tal Oved, Lior Wolf*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: Diffusion language models can write many tokens per step, but must choose which tokens are safe to commit together. Mean-field decoding penalizes pairs of positions whose predictions look alike. We replace this proxy with the coupling itself: how much committing one token changes the model's confidence at another. Cheap probes inside the main forward pass measure it, with no training or extra model, and it plugs into the same mean-field rule. On three models and four benchmarks, our method needs far fewer steps than mean-field decoding and is faster in most settings, also with a key-value cache, at an average accuracy cost of 1.1 points. Shuffling the couplings or replacing them with a uniform bonus loses this gain.


### [332] [Archetypal contexts in the neurons of language models: an architecture for intelligence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07234)
*David Nordfors*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: We validate our previous hypothesis that a language model holds relational patterns apart from the subject domains that instantiate them (Nordfors, 2026). These archetypal contexts are patterns of relations that people recognize across many fields, such as the tipping point or winner takes all, held once and applied across domains. We show this inside the model, Qwen3.8-27B. One set of neurons carries the archetype, say the tipping point; another carries the topic, say politics, ecology or cell biology; a third carries the language, such as English, Spanish or Chinese. Silence the tipping point's neurons, and the model no longer identifies the pattern but still knows what the text is about. Silence the topic's, and it loses the topic but still names the pattern. In the neuron maps the layers organize themselves into a sandwich: the outer layers carry languages, the layers inside them topics, the layers at the heart archetypes. The sandwich emerges spontaneously during pretraining and appears in every model we mapped. Archetypes are the architecture for analogy, and we strongly suggest that this structure of archetypes and topics is the mechanism of a language model's intelligence: we see it self-organize as the model is trained. The neurons, the texts and the code are released with this paper as its reproduction package, so that anyone can repeat the test inside the model.


### [333] [Encoding and Propagation of Grammatical Number in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07283)
*Jacob Solinsky, Serguei Pakhomov*

Main category: cs.CL

Abstract: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) have become an increasingly popular means of translating Large Language Model (LLM) hidden state vectors into a human-comprehensible list of features or symbols. As such, SAEs may provide insight into an LLM's knowledge representation and internal ``mental'' grammar - topics of pragmatic interest for mechanistic interpretation of AI behavior and of theoretical interest for the study of in silico representations of human language. Language models with fewer than 100M parameters, trained on small corpora, nonetheless exhibit strong grammatical competence and discourse coherence, despite producing content of limited reliability. We use SAEs that we trained on one such model (Pythia-70M) to conduct an in-depth examination of how a single grammatical phenomenon, number agreement, is represented and processed in an LLM. We demonstrate the mechanism and the methodology by which SAEs can be used to find and propagate ``plural noun'', ``plural matrix subject'', and ``generate a plural verb'' features which cause the LLM to produce plural verb forms such as ``are'', ``do'', or ``have'' instead of ``is'', ``does'', and ``has'' in response to prompts such as ``the men in the park''. We show that intervening to remove these features from the hidden states of plural subject noun tokens causes the model to produce singular verbs instead, and vice versa. We also show that corresponding ``singular noun'' and ``generate a singular verb'' features are not as effective for this task.
